## 1 Introduction

Automated machine learnin<sub>g</sub> (AutoML) automates <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>mos</sub>t <sub>es</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bli<sub>s</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>ems searc</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>ope</sub>r<sub>a</sub>t<sub>o</sub>r <sub>a</sub>nd h<sub>ype</sub>r<sub>pa</sub>r<sub>a</sub>m<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub>r <sub>space.</sub> S<sub>ys</sub>t<sub>e</sub>m<sub>s</sub> <sub>suc</sub>h <sub>as</sub> A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>Gl<sub>uon-</sub>T<sub>a</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ar,</sub> H2O A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>ML<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>au</sub>t<sub>o-s</sub>kl<sub>earn op-</sub> ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze cura</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t<sub>s o</sub>f <sub>preprocessors,</sub> l<sub>earners, an</sub>d h<sub>yper-</sub> <sub>p</sub>arameters (Erickson et al. 2020; LeDell and Poirier 2020; Feurer et al. 2015), while evolutionar<sub>y</sub> s<sub>y</sub>stems such as TPOT var<sub>y</sub> structures within a fixed o<sub>p</sub>erator inventor<sub>y</sub> (Olson et al. 2016). Tabular foundation models instead avoid <sub>p</sub>er-task search b<sub>y</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> a <sub>p</sub>retrained <sub>p</sub>redictor (Hollmann et al. 2025). Because <sub>p</sub>re<sub>p</sub>rocessin<sub>g</sub>, model, and h<sub>yp</sub>er<sub>p</sub>arameter choices interact, jointly optimizing them is dificult (Baratchi et al. 2024). ## Contributions.

1. Evolutionary AutoML over executable programs. W<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce,</sub> t<sub>o our</sub> k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t LLM<sub>-</sub>d<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ven</sub> <sub>popu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>ML f<sub>ramewor</sub>k th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>ves</sub> <sub>com-</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>programs.</sub>

2. Open code instead of a fixed grammar. We replace a <sub>pre</sub>d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>li<sub>ne grammar w</sub>ith <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ooses</sub> <sub>preprocess</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> l<sub>earners,</sub> <sub>ensem</sub>bl<sub>es,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>expose</sub>d <sup>h</sup>yp<sup>er</sup>p<sup>arameters</sup>.

3. Inspectable and editable task-specific programs. We <sub>au</sub>dit <sub>a</sub>ll fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>programs</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ow</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t LACE <sub>exposes</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>lli<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>or</sub>di<sub>nary</sub> <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e</sub> i<sub>nspec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, mo</sub>difi<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>reuse</sub>d <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e searc</sub>h f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>.</sub>

## 2 Related Work

AutoML and pipeline search. Classical AutoML often f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on as</sub> th<sub>e com</sub>bi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>a</sub>l<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>m se-</sub> l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>er op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>bl<sub>em: c</sub>h<sub>oos</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>among</sub> <sub>pre</sub>d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d l<sub>earners</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>preprocessors</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>ers, as</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o-</sub>WEKA <sub>an</sub>d commonl<sub>y</sub> o<sub>p</sub>timized with en<sub>g</sub>ines such as SMAC (Thornt<sub>on e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> ${ \dot { 2 } } 0 1 { \dot { 3 } } ;$ H<sub>u</sub>tt<sub>er,</sub> H<sub>oos, an</sub>d L<sub>ey</sub>t<sub>on-</sub>B<sub>rown</sub> 2011<sub>;</sub> Li<sub>n-</sub> dauer et al. 2022). Auto-sklearn adds meta-learnin<sub>g</sub> and ensemble construction (Feurer et al. 2015); AutoGluon and H2O AutoML use stacked ensembles (Erickson et al. 2020; LeDell and Poirier 2020); and FLAML <sub>p</sub>erforms cost-fru<sub>g</sub>al search (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2021). Evolutionar<sub>y</sub> s<sub>y</sub>stems such as TPOT<sub>,</sub> GAMA<sub>,</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd RECIPE <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>ve</sub> <sub>p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>lin<sub>es</sub> b<sub>y</sub> <sub>co</sub>m<sub>pos-</sub> in<sub>g p</sub>redefined <sub>p</sub>re<sub>p</sub>rocessin<sub>g</sub> and learnin<sub>g</sub> o<sub>p</sub>erators (Olson et al. 2016; Gijsbers and Vanschoren 2019; de Sá et al. 2017), <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>ML<sub>-</sub>Z<sub>ero cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>s</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng a</sub>l<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>ms</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> low-level scalar, vector, and matrix o<sub>p</sub>erations (Real et al. 2020). This <sub>p</sub>rovides broad ex<sub>p</sub>ressivit<sub>y</sub>, but useful learners <sub>mus</sub>t b<sub>e assem</sub>bl<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> fi<sub>ne-gra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>opera</sub>ti<sub>ons ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as co</sub>m<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> libr<sub>a</sub>r<sub>y co</sub>m<sub>po</sub>n<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>s.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bPFN r<sub>ep</sub>r<sub>ese</sub>nt<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>nc</sub>t <sub>a</sub>lt<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>ML<sub>:</sub> it <sub>app</sub>li<sub>es</sub> a <sub>p</sub>retrained <sub>p</sub>redictor without <sub>p</sub>er-task <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline search (Hollmann et al. 2023, 2025). Existin<sub>g</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>roaches therefore either <sub>searc</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n pre</sub>d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>componen</sub>t lib<sub>rar</sub>i<sub>es, cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>go-</sub> <sub>r</sub>ith<sub>ms</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> l<sub>ow-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>opera</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ace</sub> <sub>per-</sub>t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>searc</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>pre</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub>

LLM-guided evolutionary program search. LLM-<sub>gu</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d <sub>program searc</sub>h <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>es an</sub>d <sub>mu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es execu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>programs</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>are score</sub>d <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y.</sub> F<sub>un</sub>S<sub>earc</sub>h <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>ves</sub> short functions within a fixed skeleton (Romera-Paredes et al. 2024), EoH and ReEvo evolve o<sub>p</sub>timization heuristics (Liu et al. 2024; Ye et al. 2024), and Al<sub>p</sub>haEvolve extends the <sub>p</sub>attern to lar<sub>g</sub>er codebases (Novikov et al. 2025). Most closel<sub>y</sub>, Guided Evolution has an LLM <sub>p</sub>erf<sub>orm mu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>crossover over</sub> th<sub>e co</sub>d<sub>e o</sub>f <sub>a see</sub>d <sub>neura</sub>l network within a multi-objective loop (Morris, Jurado, and Zutt<sub>y</sub> 2024), thou<sub>g</sub>h it refines one architecture on a sin<sub>g</sub>le <sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>.</sub> I<sub>n</sub> th<sub>ese se</sub>tti<sub>ngs,</sub> th<sub>e evo</sub>l<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>program</sub> i<sub>s</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf <sub>a so</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ure.</sub> LACE i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>ves a</sub> l<sub>earner</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> fitt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a an</sub>d <sub>score</sub>d <sub>on</sub> h<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>-ou</sub>t d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a, ma</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> <sub>genera</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on, assessmen</sub>t l<sub>ea</sub>k<sub>age,</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k <sub>con</sub>t<sub>am</sub>i<sub>na-</sub> tion (Bordt et al. 2024; Ronval and Du<sub>p</sub>ont 2025; Silvestri et al. 2025), and dele<sub>g</sub>ation to nested AutoML central con-<sub>cerns.</sub> Th<sub>e cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ace, coarse</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>summary, an</sub>d <sub>no-nes</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-</sub>A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>o</sub>ML <sub>ru</sub>l<sub>e a</sub>dd<sub>ress</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> dif<sub>erences.</sub>

Algorithm 1 LACE evolutionary search   
1: Input: budget B, parents µ, ofspring λ, mutation in  
structions M   
2: P ← ∅   
3: for i = 1 to µ do   
4: x ← LLM(task + interface + format)   
5<sub>:</sub> $f ( x ) \gets \mathrm { E v a L U A T E } ( x )$ {record exposed space; de  
faults used in this stud<sub>y</sub>}   
6<sub>:</sub> $P  P \cup \{ x \}$   
7: end for   
8<sub>:</sub> $x ^ { \star } \gets \arg \operatorname* { m a x } _ { x \in P } f ( x )$   
9: while evaluated candidates $< B$ do   
10<sub>:</sub> O ← ∅   
11<sub>:</sub> for j = 1 to λ do   
12<sub>:</sub> p ← uniform random <sub>p</sub>arent from $P$   
13<sub>:</sub> m ← uniform random instruction from M   
14<sub>:</sub> x ← LLM(task + summary(P) + p +   
feedbac $\therefore ( p ) + m +$ format)   
15<sub>:</sub> f(x) ← Evaluate(x)   
16<sub>:</sub> $O  O \cup \{ x \}$   
17<sub>:</sub> end for   
18<sub>:</sub> $P $ best µ of $P \cup O$ {elitist truncation}   
19<sub>:</sub> if m $\operatorname { a x } _ { x \in P } { \dot { f } } ( x ) > f ( x ^ { \star } )$ then   
20<sub>:</sub> $x ^ { \star } \gets \arg \operatorname* { m a x } _ { x \in P } f ( x )$   
21<sub>:</sub> end if   
22: end while   
23: return x<sup>⋆</sup>; refit and evaluate once per oficial test fold

Ofspring count and parent selection. Increasing the ofspring count from 4 to 12 (at µ = 4) improved developmentset performance, so we adopt λ = 12. Selection pressure. Selection shifted the composition toward tree ensembles. Gradient boosting rose from 59.6% of valid candidates to 69.4% of final pipelines, and forests or extra trees rose from 43.1% to 52.5%, while linear models fell from 62.8% to 55.8%. The shift was strongest under GPT-5.4-mini, for which forests increased by 18.3 percentage points and linear models decreased by 15.5 points. Illustrative inspectability example. A source-level adjudication identified 89 of the 1,020 finals (8.7%, spanning 53 tasks) that combine <sub>p</sub>redictions directl<sub>y</sub> in NumP<sub>y</sub> or <sub>or</sub>di<sub>nary</sub> P<sub>y</sub>th<sub>on.</sub> Thi<sub>s ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e reac</sub>h<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d i<sub>nven-</sub> t<sub>ory o</sub>f 75 h<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-</sub>b<sub>u</sub>ilt <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ons repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>a</sub>b<sub>ove: manua</sub>l <sub>rev</sub>i<sub>ew</sub> f<sub>oun</sub>d 14 <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>-</sub> <sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y pass m</sub>i<sub>sse</sub>d<sub>, o</sub>f <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h 82 <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> 89 <sub>sa</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>f<sub>y a</sub>ll <sub>au</sub>dit<sub>e</sub>d <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> A<sub>mong</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>an</sub>t fi<sub>na</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>eares</sub>t <sub>compac</sub>t <sub>source</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>mary</sub> GPT<sub>-</sub>5<sub>.</sub>4<sub>-</sub> <sub>m</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one</sub> f<sub>or</sub> ill<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>program</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> final <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline for O<sub>p</sub>enML task 2073, re<sub>p</sub>etition 4 (candidate 0ce14317-05e0-4fed-9eb4-3b7852b46cb2). It obtained oficial-test score 0.6206 and final inner-validation fitness 0.6263; its traced lineage improved from 0.5964 to 0.6203 to 0.6263. The intermediate candidate introduced h<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-wr</sub>itt<sub>en</sub> l<sub>og</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c-regress</sub>i<sub>on an</sub>d E<sub>x</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>T<sub>rees pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> averaging with weights 0.55/0.45; the final candidate <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ace</sub>d th<sub>ese w</sub>ith <sub>an</sub> SVC <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> f<sub>ores</sub>t <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t 0.60/0.40.

```python
def __call__(self, X):
Xp = self._transform(X)
p_svc = self.svc.predict_proba(Xp)
p_rf = self.rf.predict_proba(Xp)
p = 0.60 <sub>*</sub> p_svc + 0.40 <sub>*</sub> p_rf
return np.argmax(p, axis=1)
```

Final pipeline interface. Listing 1 shows the interface of th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>li<sub>ne se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> thi<sub>s represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve run.</sub>

```python
class
TabularEnsemblePipeline(BaseEstimator,
ClassifierMixin):
def __init__(self, X, y, <sub>**</sub>hp):
# fit once, no CV
self.pre_ =
self._build_preprocessor()
Xt =
self.pre_.fit_transform(self._to_df(X))
self.model = VotingClassifier(
voting="soft",
weights=(1.0, 1.0, 1.15),
estimators=[
("lr",
LogisticRegression(...)),
("et",
ExtraTreesClassifier(...)),
("hgb",
HistGradientBoostingClassifier(...)),
],
)
self.model.fit(Xt, y)
def __call__(self, X):
# transform, then predict
Xt =
self.pre_.transform(self._to_df(X))
return self.model.predict(Xt)
```

You are an excellent Python programmer.   
You can use the following Python packages:   
scikit-learn, numpy, scipy, pandas.   
Design an ML pipeline for a {task\_type} task   
with {samples\_desc} and   
{feats\_desc}.   
Write a single Python class:   
- \_\_init\_\_(self, X, y, <sub>\*\*</sub>hyperparameters)   
-> fit exactly once (no CV here)   
\_\_call\_\_(self, X)   
IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS (must follow):   
- Do NOT use any internal HPO or CV search (   
no GridSearchCV,   
RandomizedSearchCV, BayesSearchCV, Optuna,   
Hyperopt, skopt, etc.).   
- Do NOT implement your own tuning loops (no   
KFold/StratifiedKFold loops, no   
parameter sweeps).   
Pick ONE primary estimator OBJECT inside   
the class. This can be either (a) a   
single sklearn estimator, OR (b) a simple   
sklearn ensemble wrapper (e.g.,   
VotingClassifier or StackingClassifier)   
that combines up to 2-3 base

```python
estimators.
Do NOT do estimator-switching in a param
grid.
- If you use an ensemble wrapper, keep it
small (2-3 models max), and keep
preprocessing shared (apply preprocessing
once, then feed the same
transformed X to all base estimators).
If you do preprocessing (e.g.,
StandardScaler, PCA), assign them to
self.<sub>*</sub>
and reuse them in __call__.
Expose tunable hyperparameters via
_init__ kwargs with sensible defaults;
Here is a minimal template (for reference
only; you must output your own
improved class). An example code structure
is as follows:
‘‘‘python
import numpy as np
import sklearn
from sklearn.preprocessing import
StandardScaler
from sklearn.linear_model import
LogisticRegression
class MyPipeline:
"Simple, single-estimator pipeline
without internal HPO."
def __init__(self, X, y, C=1.0):
# keep references for use in
__call
self.scaler = StandardScaler()
Xs = self.scaler.fit_transform(X)
# choose ONE estimator; expose its
hyperparameters
self.model = LogisticRegression(C=C,
max_iter=1000, n_jobs=1,
random_state=42)
self.model.fit(Xs, y)
def __call__(self, X):
Xs = self.scaler.transform(X)
return self.model.predict(Xs)
```
